Dominant flat silhouettes emerge from O-Yoshi's black kimono mass, the ghost's white washi cutout, and the horizontal veranda rail, with crisp overlap where her silver-edged sleeve crosses her hip and where the ghost's painted face partially overlaps the upper strip's dark zone; parallel limbs and fabric folds run horizontally across the ōban width, broken by one diagonal from her left shoulder through her supporting forearm to the sake cup below. The five-color hierarchy places sumi black in kimono, obi shadow, and ghost outline; orpiment and vermilion warm the sake cup, cloth napkin, and a bounded blush accent across O-Yoshi's exposed breast and cheek; unbleached-washi pale grounds the ghost body, bamboo slats, and moon edge; malachite activates distant bamboo leaves in the upper strip's left corner; all remain contour-enclosed matte fields with the bokashi gradient strictly above, never touching skin. Close-read cues include O-Yoshi's mature heart-shaped face structure with individualized jaw contour and the visible seam where her black kimono's silver paulownia pattern registers across breast and hip folds; physical craft appears in the ghost's actual washi fiber texture and the lacquer cup's burnished rim, while localized patina shows silver paulownia oxidized to grey at shoulder folds where workshop light fell, abraded bamboo paler where veranda meets garden steps from decades of foot traffic, and the kimono's lower hem softened from repeated workshop kneeling. The complete image remains a flat, borderless, three-band nishiki-e theatrical picture plane; adult performers are recognizable only through mature faces, coherent joints, hand tasks, support, and contact, while every form stays within matte contour fields on one shallow picture surface.

Adult faces, limbs, garments, and furniture read as contour-enclosed matte color shapes with crisp overlaps and two-step values; contour and flat shadow shapes carry all form. Edo-period material patina appears only as bounded wear on physical costumes, props, and set surfaces. O-Yoshi sits at image-right on the veranda edge with her torso turned toward image-left, her pelvis angled to present her back partially to the plane, her left hand gripping the veranda rail behind her while her right hand reaches upward toward the drooping ghost's painted face; her weight rests on her left hip and spread left knee with her right leg folded beneath, foot tucked against the bamboo. The current erotic but non-explicit interaction is O-Yoshi's laughing reclamation of her own exposed body as she reaches to flick the paper ghost away, her black kimono fallen completely from both shoulders to bare her back and the curve of her buttocks to the night air while her upward reach lifts one breast into full view, her expression triumphant and mocking toward her own creation.
